# Spec — drafting LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 (PROPOSAL-m v1.11 §8 step 9)

Repo: /Users/jasondwyer/Documents/code/jspace-lab. "m:N" = line N of PROPOSAL-m-gating-campaign-v1_11.md (long lines; read with python and quote exactly).

## What this is

LOCK-m-F0 v0.1 is Q-F0's first lock. The PI has given the word ("Go for step 9: draft and commit LOCK-m-F0 v0.1"), and the design seat made it Code's to draft and commit ("LOCK v0.1 is then Code's to draft and commit on the PI's word"). It is written by Code, from the record. It adds no new reading: every reading in it is either the seat's ruling (quoted, with its file and item) or Code's PIN already declared in code or in an entry. Where Code must choose a form (layout, wording), the choice is Code's and says so.

File: `LOCK-m-F0.md` at the repo root. Later versions (v0.2, m:282; v1.0, m:286) amend this same file, and the v0.1 record stays below them, as LOCK-h.md does. A stage cites it by `identity_extra` `lock_file` = "LOCK-m-F0.md" and `lock_sha256` = the sha256 of the committed file.

**The truncation rule (hard).** Check (f) in `stages.licence_problems` and the analyzer's PIN-14 test whether the lock's text contains `analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256` (and, for the analyzer, `closure_sha256`) as substrings. So the lock must contain the analyzer, package and closure sha256 above in full, and it must not contain any other analyzer or package sha256 in full. That covers the superseded analyzer 24d44a3c…, every historical package (96fbcacf…, 4b496587…, 06ab1430…), and the set-aside runs' analyzers and packages (ac637232…/750b24ed…, 0da9115b…/12652f39…, 8e709582…/19edfcd0…). Write those as at most the first 16 hex followed by "…".

## Style

Plain, precise English in this repo's register: dense but readable; bullets where they help; no marketing. Quote the seat character-exact, and attribute every reading to the seat, to Code, or to the proposal. Where a value comes from a file, cite the file (and line or field). Use backticks for paths, keys and hashes.

## Hard rules for every agent

- READ-ONLY on the repo, except where a task explicitly names a scratch output file under /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-jasondwyer-Documents-code-jspace-lab/adb70afc-5886-490b-a675-abb30f8c425f/scratchpad/step9/lock/. Never write under the repo. No git writes. Never run git inside jlens-qwen36/.
- Never touch the audit session's files: scripts/audit_note_*, results/raw/*note-*, README-NOTE.md, RESEARCH-NOTE-*, AUDIT-note-*, LICENS*, root *.svg. Never touch ADDENDUM-k1.md.
- No model or tokenizer loads. Python: /Users/jasondwyer/Documents/code/jspace-lab/jlens-qwen36/.venv/bin/python, with -I for scratch scripts; put the repo root on sys.path explicitly. The Bash tool's grep is ugrep, so use /usr/bin/grep.
- Compute every hash and count yourself with a tool before writing it. Never invent a value; if one cannot be found, write "[UNRESOLVED: what and why]".

  * [minor -> refuter: CONFIRMED, minor] The reason given for 24 distinct banners names only one of the two coincidences
    LOCATION:
      draft_lock.md §7, lines 895–896
    CLAIM:
      The text explains 24 distinct strings by one A0s coincidence: (map 0, clause 1) = (map 1, clause 0). There are two
      per pair. With only one, A0s would give 12 distinct strings and the total would be 28, not 24.
    EVIDENCE:
      With banner.build_banner('A0s', P, Q, c) on a roster pair, both (map 0, clause 1) == (map 1, clause 0) and (map 0,
      clause 0) == (map 1, clause 1) are True. Counting gives 32 banners, 24 distinct: 16 on A0 and 8 on A0s.
    PROPOSED FIX:
      Change to: "because on A0s, (map 0, clause 1) and (map 1, clause 0) write the same line, as do (map 0, clause 0) and
      (map 1, clause 1): 16 distinct on A0 and 8 on A0s".
    REFUTER:
      I checked this myself against valence_core/m/banner.py and schedule.py, read-only, with a scratch script
      (chk_banner_refute.py, run with -I and no model loads). build_banner fills TOLD_MAP_SHAM[clause] with L_a=P and
      L_b=Q, and P = pair[map], Q = pair[1-map] (schedule.py:13, :204). Clause 1 is clause 0 with the two label clauses
      swapped (banner.py:41-48). So for a pair (X, Y) on A0s:
      - (m0,c0) gives "X; Y"
      - (m0,c1) gives "Y; X"
      - (m1,c0) gives "Y; X"
      - (m1,c1) gives "X; Y"
      
      That makes two coincidences per pair, not one. I ran this on the Q-F0 roster (granite/plaza, prism/slate,
      velvet/walnut, ridge/saddle) with templates A0 and A0s:
      - There are 32 banners and 24 are distinct.
      - A0 has 16 distinct banners. Neither coincidence holds for any pair.
      - A0s has 8 distinct banners. Both (m0,c1)==(m1,c0) and (m0,c0)==(m1,c1) are True for all four pairs.
      
      With only the one coincidence the draft names, A0s would give 3 per pair, so 12, and the total would be 28. The
      draft's stated reason therefore does not produce its own count of 24. The 32/24 figures and the digest
      968e99a1...91bd are right; I recomputed the digest and it matches. Only the reason given is incomplete. The
      reviewer's claim is correct and the proposed fix checks out against the code.
    EXACT FIX:
      In draft_lock.md §7 (lines 895–896), replace:
      
      "They are 24 distinct byte strings,
      because on A0s, (map 0, clause 1) and (map 1, clause 0) write the same line."
      
      with:
      
      "They are 24 distinct byte strings,
      because on A0s, within each pair, (map 0, clause 1) and (map 1, clause 0) write the same line, as do (map 0, clause
      0) and (map 1, clause 1): 16 distinct on A0 and 8 on A0s."

      `resume.json_text` (`resume.py:171–176`) is `json.dumps(obj, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False, default=_refuse,
      allow_nan=False, indent=indent) + "\n"`. G-SHAPE-F0 computes the input at `gshape.py:1469` and `:2006`.
      
      The draft calls the hash "the sha256 of their sort-keyed JSON list text". That drops the trailing newline, so a
      reader who follows the text gets `d227a3e5…`, not the value the draft quotes. The draft's own value is correct; only
      the description of how it is serialised is wrong.

    LOCATION:
      draft_lock.md §6 'The gate-stage launch order', G-ID bullet, lines 868–870
    CLAIM:
      "A seventeenth index STOPs (`schedule.py:256`, P173)" reads as the G-ID outcome. The driver never asks for a
      seventeenth index. Past j = 15 without two complete identical pairs, G-ID ends NOT-DETERMINED, which sends Q-F0
      uncached, not a STOP. §5.1 (lines 695–696) of the same draft says NOT-DETERMINED.
    EVIDENCE:
      - stages.py:1703: verdict = "NOT-DETERMINED" if len(pairs) >= _schedule.GATE_MAX.
      - schedule.py:74: GATE_MAX = 16.
      - stages.py:638–641 (PIN): "a FAIL or NOT-DETERMINED sends Q-F0 uncached, m:281".
      - schedule.py:256's PIN is a guard in the gate-schedule builder.
    PROPOSED FIX:
      Change to: "…and no row exists beyond j = 15: past j = 15 without two complete identical pairs, G-ID ends
      NOT-DETERMINED (`gid_decide`, stages.py:1687, :1703), which sends Q-F0 uncached (stages.py:638–641, PIN);
      `schedule.py:256` (P173) refuses a seventeenth row as a guard."
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds, and I checked it against the working tree. The valence_core/m files have no uncommitted changes.
      
      - **The driver stops at sixteen.** `run_gid_pair` loops `for j in range(len(cells))` (stages.py:1759). Its cells
      come from the committed `data/m/schedules/Q-F0_id_A0s.json`, which has 16 cells. After each pair it breaks once
      `gid_decide` returns anything other than OPEN (stages.py:1782).
      - **Sixteen pairs give NOT-DETERMINED, not STOP.** `gid_decide` returns NOT-DETERMINED once `len(pairs) >=
      _schedule.GATE_MAX` (stages.py:1703; GATE_MAX = 16 at schedule.py:74). Its docstring says "past j = 15 without 2 ->
      NOT-DETERMINED (never PASS)" (stages.py:1687). So the driver never asks for a seventeenth index.
      - **A STOP comes from somewhere else.** In `_gid_end` a STOP comes only from a bar hit. NOT-DETERMINED is an end
      status of its own.
      - **NOT-DETERMINED sends Q-F0 uncached.** `guard.cached_path_admitted` admits the cached path only for a PASS
      artifact (guard.py:201 via `_gid_pass`). The PIN at stages.py:638–641 says "a FAIL or NOT-DETERMINED sends Q-F0
      uncached, m:281".
      - **The draft contradicts itself.** Its §5.1 (line 696) already says NOT-DETERMINED.
      
      The draft did not invent the word STOP. P173's own comment (schedule.py:256–257) says "a gate stage that would need
      a seventeenth session (G-ID past sixteen indices) STOPs". But what that code does is a ValueError refusal in
      `gate_schedule` for any m outside 1..16 (schedule.py:258–259), and the G-ID driver never reaches it. Read as G-ID's
      outcome, the draft's sentence is wrong.
      
      The reviewer's fix is accurate. I extend it to say how P173's word "STOPs" relates to the actual behaviour. The lock
      declares that PIN, so a reader who compares the two should not find them in conflict.
    EXACT FIX:
      In draft_lock.md §6, 'The gate-stage launch order', G-ID-F0m bullet (lines 868–870), replace:

          i = j = line_no(p) - 1

  * [minor -> refuter: CONFIRMED, minor] "A Qwen stage's identity should carry that value" reads as a new lock requirement
    LOCATION:
      draft_lock.md:922-926 (lock §7, "No separate sha16")
    CLAIM:
      The `ban_sha` value `a934f1ad2b702245` is Code's computation, carried by no record (lock §11 lists it so). In a

    EVIDENCE:
      - Line 924: "Computed by Code for this lock from the file's ids (no record carries it yet), it is

      list equals the file."
    PROPOSED FIX:
      Replace the last sentence with: "Code expects a Qwen stage's identity to carry that value, since `qwen_ban_list`
      asserts that the live list equals the file. Nothing compares a stage's `ban_sha` with this lock, and the value binds
      nothing."
    REFUTER:
      The finding holds. I checked it against the code and the draft.
      
      - **The value is correct.** sha16 of `json.dumps(sorted(ids))` over the 12 ids in `data/configs/e2a_ban_list.json`
      (sha256 9a2f0744f81ac930…, recomputed) is `a934f1ad2b702245`, recomputed. It is the form `stages._startup` uses
      (`stages.py:952–955`). The value occurs nowhere else in the repo (`/usr/bin/grep -rl` over the tree, excluding
      `.git` and `jlens-qwen36`), so "no record carries it yet" is true. Lock §11 (draft :1316–1319) lists it among the
      values Code computed that no record carries.
      - **Nothing checks it against the lock.**
        - `stages.licence_problems` (a)–(f) reads only these from the lock's identity: the proposal and lock file hashes,
      the analyzer's bytes, `package_sha256`, and the lock text's analyzer and package substrings.
        - The analyzer mentions `ban_sha` only in test fixtures (`m_analyze.py:4286`, `:4346`, `:5667`).
        - `resume.IDENTITY_KEYS` (`resume.py:93–96`) includes `ban_sha`, but a resume compares it only with the stage's
      own checkpoint, never with the lock.
      - **So "should carry" is Code's unattributed expectation, in normative-sounding words.** The expectation is sound:
      `qwen_ban_list` reads the ids from the file (`runner_qwen.py:76`; `session.py:111–113`). `assert_qwen_scaffold`
      checks them against the sha256-pinned file (`audit.py:233–243`), and the live rebuild must equal them. Any stage
      that gets past startup therefore hashes the file's ids. But the spec requires every reading to be attributed. The
      draft's other Code-computed values carry that disclaimer: the banner digest says "(Code's form, computed for this
      lock and checked by no code)" (:896–897), and the schedule_sha256 values say "each stage records its own at run
      time" (:820–821). The ban_sha sentence alone drops it.
      - **Severity.** A minor ambiguity in a binding document, not a wrong value.
      - **The reviewer's fix needs one adjustment.** It is accurate in substance, and "Nothing compares a stage's
      `ban_sha` with this lock" is true. Its reason clause gives only the live-list assertion. The fuller reason is that
      the hashed ids are read from the sha-pinned file, so the fix below states that. It also names the actual pin, so
      "binds nothing" cannot be misread.
    EXACT FIX:
      In draft_lock.md lines 924–926 (lock §7, "No separate sha16"), replace the sentence "A Qwen stage's identity should
      carry that value, because `qwen_ban_list` asserts that the live list equals the file." with:
      
      "Code expects a Qwen stage's identity to carry that value, since the ids `_startup` hashes are the file's:
      `qwen_ban_list` reads them from the file (`runner_qwen.py:76`), `assert_qwen_scaffold` checks them against the
      file's sha256, and the live list must equal them. Nothing compares a stage's `ban_sha` with this lock (a resume
      compares it only with the stage's own checkpoint), and the value binds nothing; the pin is the file's sha256 above."

      I checked the claim against the sources and it holds.
      
      1. **What line 1164 says.** It states as a fact that the analyzer the lock names (`66fb5214…7d5e`) "is the one

      form calls it "once more", but that is a plan.

      G-F0m-Qwen runs under.

--- [A-33] FAILS
  CLAIM:
    §0: "The licence by file (lock §1) checks all of these at stage start." These are lock_file, lock_sha256,
    proposal_file, proposal_sha256, renders_sha16, analyzer_sha256, package_sha256 and locked_path.
  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I checked this against HEAD 9bf36ad (valence_core/m/stages.py, identical to the working tree). The draft's §0
    defines "these" as lock_file, lock_sha256, proposal_file, proposal_sha256, renders_sha16, analyzer_sha256,
    package_sha256 and locked_path. It then says "The licence by file (lock §1) checks all of these at stage start."
    Lock §1 itself identifies the licence by file as `stages.licence_problems` (stages.py:530–608), with checks (a)–(f).
    Those checks cover the following:
    - (a) HEAD is known.
    - (b) proposal_file is committed and its sha256 equals proposal_sha256.
    - (c) lock_file is committed and its sha256 equals lock_sha256.
    - (d) The analyzer's bytes hash to analyzer_sha256.
    - (e) resume.package_sha256() equals package_sha256.
    - (f) The lock's text names both 64-hex values.
    
    licence_problems never reads renders_sha16 or locked_path, and `_licence` (:611–633) calls only licence_problems.
    renders_sha16 is checked elsewhere. `_extra` checks only its form (:1048–1049). `_renders_of_record` (:1060–1067)
    compares it against `_corr.renders_sha` of the renders built from `_sc.pool_and_renders()` and raises RENDERS
    MISMATCH if they differ. The three drivers call it right after `_licensed`, before any write (:1473, :1737, :2041).
    
    locked_path is also checked outside the licence. `_extra` (:1050–1056) requires cached or uncached, and uncached for
    G-TIME and OLMo. `_locked` (:1080–1099) compares it with the path this backend's G-ID licence gives and raises PATH
    MISMATCH unless an end is pending. It is called at :1490 and :2063, before load_or_init.
    
    So the claim is wrong for two of the eight keys. The checker's replacement is correct, and every line range it cites
    matches HEAD.
  CORRECTION:
    - At stage start the licence by file (lock §1) checks `proposal_file` and `proposal_sha256`, `lock_file` and
    `lock_sha256`, `analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256`; `renders_sha16` is checked against the renders of record
    built at stage start (`_renders_of_record`, `stages.py:1060–1067`), and `locked_path` by `_extra`
    (`stages.py:1050–1056`) and `_locked` (`stages.py:1080–1099`).

  REFUTER'S EVIDENCE:
    I re-checked this independently at HEAD 9bf36ad. Check (f) (stages.py:601–607) tests the values in `have`. Those are
    the `analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256` keys that identity_extra offers, taken only when they are well-formed 64
    lower-case hex (stages.py:578–585). Each is tested with `v not in ltext`, where ltext is the committed lock blob
    decoded as UTF-8 with errors replaced (lines 559, 564, 602). For (f), then, "offered" is correct.
    
    PIN-14 (m_analyze.py:1912–1924) is different: nothing is offered to it. It tests three values the analyzer computes
    itself:
    - `a_sha = _sha256_bytes(abytes)`, where abytes is the committed HEAD blob of the analyzer
    (`env.committed(env.analyzer_path)` goes to `stages._committed`, which runs `git cat-file blob HEAD:<rel>`, with
    working-tree bytes required to be identical).
    - `env.package_sha256`, which `production_env` sets to `resume.package_sha256()` (m_analyze.py:1766). That value is
    computed once per process over `valence_core/m/*.py` and cached (resume.py:321–328).
    - `env.closure_sha256`, which is `closure_sha256(closure())` (m_analyze.py:1767). It is recomputed in a fresh
    `python -I -B` subprocess (:1703–1718).
    
    Each of these is a substring test against the committed lock text, and the analyzer raises AnlRefusal if one is
    missing. So "the offered hash" is wrong for PIN-14. The singular "hash" is also loose: (f) tests two hashes and
    PIN-14 tests three. The iff-substring structure of the claim is right.
  CORRECTION:
    each pass iff this file's committed text contains, as a substring, every hash the check tests. For check (f), those
    are `identity_extra`'s `analyzer_sha256` and `package_sha256`, each tested when it is well-formed 64 lower-case hex.
    For PIN-14, they are the sha256 of the committed analyzer blob, the process's `resume.package_sha256()` and the
    closure sha256 the analyzer recomputes (`closure_sha256(closure())`).

--- [B-40]
    Each non-word-initial letter is replaced independently with probability 0.12 (corr.py:85), by a letter drawn from
    all 26 (may repeat the original); no k; operands eligible.
  REFUTER:
    The draft (draft_lock.md:401) reads "replaced independently with probability 0.12 (`corr.py:85`), by a letter drawn
    from all 26". The parenthetical comes straight after "probability 0.12", so it cites that value, and corr.py:85 at
    HEAD 9bf36ad is exactly that value: `F1_RATE = 0.12  # f_sessions.py:86 CORR_RATE (f1's own float, verbatim)`. The
    spec (spec_lock.md:101) asks that a value taken from a file cite the file and line, and this citation does that. The
    line above it (draft:400) already gives the rule its source, quoting the artifact's field "f1 corrupt() at 0.12,
    f_sessions.py:106-116 (26-letter draw, may repeat)", which matches `data/m/gates/G-CAL_model-free.json`
    `.f1_012.rule` and corr.py:334. Nothing in the sentence says the replacement mechanism sits at line 85.
    
    Each part of the behaviour checks out:
    - **Eligibility and rate:** corrupt_f1 (corr.py:276-286) and f1's own corrupt()
    (jlens-qwen36/scripts/f_sessions.py:106-116) test `ch.isalpha() and not word_start and rng.random() < F1_RATE` and
    then set `word_start = not ch.isalpha()`. So only letters that do not begin a word are eligible, and each gets its
    own Bernoulli draw at 0.12.
    - **Replacement draw:** the replacement is `chr(ord('a') + int(rng.integers(26)))`, one of all 26 letters, so it can
    equal the original. The artifact shows this happening: n_hits 32 against n_changed 31.
    - **No k, operands eligible:** the PIN at corr.py:325-327 reads "f1 has no k ... non-word-start letters, operands
    included". f1's rule also has no quote exclusion, unlike `_eligible_m` (corr.py:187-201).
    
    The checker's correction adds a second citation (corrupt_f1, corr.py:276-286). That makes the sentence fuller, but
    it does not fix an error, because the citation as drafted is correct for the value it is attached to.

--- (Code deciding what is the seat's)
  QUOTE:
    If the PI wants the question answered before the run, it goes to the seat through the PI before
  PROBLEM:
    The lock flags the question as the seat's (does a post-lock edit to a file in G-SHAPE-F0's tooling map fall under
    D3's 'any other difference re-runs G-SHAPE-F0 first'?). But the default it sets acts on one answer before the seat
    gives it. The seat reads the lock only at the step-10 ping, which comes after the run, so Code would wire
    `scripts/m_sessions.py` and launch G-TIME-F0 without re-running G-SHAPE-F0 unless the PI intervenes. R1 A4's
    going-forward rule says a step that follows a gate starts only after the PI's go names the open items and the seat
    has said they cannot change the step's inputs. R1 A2 adds that a departing reading is put to the seat before Code
    acts on it. As drafted, the burden shifts to the PI and Code acts on the reading 'no re-run'.
  CORRECTION:
    Replace the last two sentences of the 'Flagged for the seat' bullet with: "Launching G-TIME-F0 on the edited driver
    without re-running G-SHAPE-F0 would act on the answer 'no' before the seat has given it (R1 A4's and A2's
    going-forward rules). The question therefore goes to the seat through the PI with the step-10 word. Code does not
    launch G-TIME-F0 on an edited driver until the seat answers or the PI directs otherwise."
--- (lock misstates how a stage behaves)
  QUOTE:
    complete cached/uncached A0s pair (4 forced rounds + 6 reads each) | complete pairs whose transcripts differ in any
    byte
  PROBLEM:
    Bar row 5 declares the G-ID bar's unit and numerator as complete pairs only, and lock §8.3 repeats 'unit a complete
    pair'. G-ID's decision as built is different: `gid_decide` (stages.py:1682, PIN P227 at :1691) returns FAIL on the
    first non-identical pair, complete or not, and `run_gid_pair` sets `identical` regardless of completeness. This is
    build flag (e), which the seat accepted as built at `54e41c9`. So G-ID fails on an incomplete diverging pair, which
    the lock's own bar table says it does not count. §5.1 quotes the any-pair PIN as Code's and does not mention the
    acceptance, so the lock contradicts itself on when G-ID fails.
  CORRECTION:
    Below the bar table (and in §8.3's G-ID bullet), add: "Row 5's unit, numerator and cadence are `bars.py`'s
    declaration text. As built, G-ID's decision (`gid_decide`, stages.py:1682; PIN P227 at :1691) fails G-ID on a
    divergence in any pair, complete or not. Only a byte-identical incomplete pair advances j, and 'complete' counts
